Candidate presentation_runtime.s is SHA-256 6c7525fbc98e349128e58310e805aca6fd8b3a209f252445dba9e7bfcad85123; its presentation runtime module is 1,280 bytes, SHA-256 53720dd325f3db6c74259d67a987b672f2f3b277a0313ac0214073a8a9da04f2. Pinned build.sh is SHA-256 c3beb3400479144c5ed639f8f60aca8f7f1ddaac76d4d296c9a1d2ce19d85fa3.

The helper source at presentation_attract_overlay_impl computes the desired actor phase, compares it with PRES_ACTOR_PHASE, then writes the new phase and calls PRES_MAIN_FB_PREPARE before copying actors. The same helper already defers work while PENDING is nonzero: pht_copy tests PENDING before its copy, and pht_publish tests PENDING before restoring framebuffer ownership. This is a source pattern for admission, not proof that PENDING caused A1.

Bounded A1 hypothesis and proposed one-file delta

Hypothesis: if the desired actor phase differs while PENDING is set, the overlay should preserve the old PRES_ACTOR_PHASE and return before beginning another BACK transaction. The next normal Attract dispatch can recompute the phase and admit it after PENDING clears. This avoids advancing the phase cache before admission. It neither clears PENDING nor waits in a loop, and it does not guarantee a visible repair or replay every intermediate phase.

The only proposed production file is the fresh candidate's src/perimeter_reset_helper.s. Insert direct-page `tst PENDING` and short `bne pao_done` on the phase-change path, before `sta PRES_ACTOR_PHASE` and before `jsr PRES_MAIN_FB_PREPARE`. Use the existing same-helper PENDING admission style. Keep the actor copy and finish/capture path unchanged. No change has been applied yet.

The helper allocation is $06B2-$07FF inclusive (334 bytes; exclusive end $0800). The current image occupies [$06B2,$07FD), 331 bytes; it leaves three bytes at $07FD-$07FF. The proposed TST and BNE cost four bytes. Recover exactly one byte by changing `lbsr presentation_attract_phase` at $0786 to `bsr presentation_attract_phase`. Current bytes are 17 00 5B and target $07E4. After both edits, the callee moves to $07E7 and the BSR return address is $0788, giving projected signed displacement +95 ($5F), within the 6809 BSR range -128..+127. Projected BSR bytes are 8D 5F. The guard at $078C is projected as 0D 91 26 53: direct TST of PENDING=$91, then BNE from $0790 to pao_done at $07E3. The phase store moves to $0790 and the prepare JSR to $0792. Net growth is three bytes; projected helper size is exactly 334/334 and its exclusive end is $0800. The listing and compiled bytes must confirm every address and displacement; these are not yet built facts.

BSR and LBSR are both relative subroutine calls and both push a return address for RTS. A compiled postcheck must decode the actual BSR target and return path, not assume the target stayed at $07E4. The BNE destination must decode to pao_done. The helper has zero spare bytes after the proposed change. Any source drift, different instruction width, changed target, branch out of range, or size other than 334 stops the step. Do not widen the allocation, relocate unrelated code, or edit the full presentation runtime, which is already 1,280/1,280 bytes.

FEAT-010 shared-cause diagnosis — 2026-10-18 Status: report only; no runtime or production action authorized by this step.

Conclusion

The evidence does not establish that Attract stutter and menu-selection rollback share a cause. Static source supports a queued-publication delay that can defer a menu repaint, and independently shows every Attract menu event resets the actor clock. The prior no-input run observed a pending interval at the actor boundary, not its producer, commit, visible frame, or rollback. The A1 actor-admission guard does not change menu ordering or prove A2 behavior.

Pinned evidence and limits

The inspected artifact is immutable candidate ROM `repro/feat010-frontend-dispatch-repair-20261016/candidate/build/ladybug.rom`, SHA-256 `6b2181bfba91869fcfe304524ecdd965d30c7b5ad6c560c0b00da67439282b08`, with source-build receipt SHA-256 `cf861fcaeb95c1c905d758d0d7a191a128dd4c56ecf2a6a00e4e643db464c0e0`. The retained complete-profile no-input run `repro/feat010-attract-next-diagnosis-20261017/runtime-result-20261018.json` byte-qualified its planned spans before reading direct-page state.

At `$1C56`, PENDING was 0; at the distinct `$B830` callback entry, PENDING remained 0 and MENU_EVENT/SELECT/DIRTY were 0. At `$078D`, PENDING was 1, actor phase was still 3, and menu event/selection/dirty were still 0. The run ended there. It did not observe the same-callback PENDING producer, a later prepare/finish/IRQ commit or FRONT write, input, or visible output. `target_path_prequalified=false` and the run's interpretation limit remain controlling. No visual FRONT capture exists; the displayed-FRONT observer is unqualified.

Source-only findings

In `src/shared_text_stage.inc`, `highscore_attract_tick` calls `highscore_logo_tick` before `menu_tick` (lines 1306–1311). The logo path checks PENDING and may prepare/finish a redraw. `menu_tick` can then change MENU_SELECT and set MENU_DIRTY; `menu_idle` defers its painter when PENDING is set. In `src/presentation_runtime.s`, Attract calls the page-23 callback before the actor overlay. The actor overlay stores a changed phase and calls prepare without checking PENDING. `framebuffer_prepare_back` checks initialization and BACK/FRONT ownership but not PENDING; `framebuffer_finish_back` captures and sets PENDING. This is a credible shared publication-delay candidate. It does not identify a runtime producer or prove a visible rollback.

A separate clock candidate is source-supported: `attract_tick` increments PRES_TIMER; an Attract MENU_EVENT clears it; `presentation_attract_phase` derives actor phase from PRES_TIMER. This may restart actor animation during navigation, independently of whether menu painting is deferred. No input run has established the reported stutter. MENU_SELECT also clears on `menu_change`, so an exact screen/input sequence is needed to distinguish an expected screen transition from a logical reversal.

A1 guard boundary

The approved A1 check defers actor phase storage and prepare while PENDING is set. It does not reorder logo and menu callbacks, change menu edge/selection/dirty state, prevent the Attract timer reset, or prove FRONT pixels. It cannot close A2 coverage.

2026-10-18 — Menu reset prioritized; timestamp design under review

User prioritizes navigation-induced animation reset before the unresolved no-input stutter and requests design/code impacts. This supersedes the proposed preparation order only: menu design may proceed first; no runtime, production repair or broad no-input diagnosis is authorized. Parent invokes coding-architect and assigns Luna Extra High a read-only bounded design review. High-risk class remains because clock lifetime and full module allocations are involved.

Confirmed path: presentation_runtime.s attract_tick increments PRES_TIMER, calls the page-$23 title/menu callback, then checks idle expiry and calls the actor overlay. shared_text_stage.inc clears both PRES_TIMER bytes after an Attract menu event. perimeter_reset_helper.s computes the actor phase from PRES_TIMER minus6, with three shifts and a two-bit mask; phase3 displays frame1. Thus the existing menu event resets the input to the actor phase calculation. PRES_ACTOR_PHASE is a render cache, not an independent elapsed clock; merely retaining that byte cannot preserve subsequent phase timing. The logo already uses a separate IRQ-atomic VBLANK clock and its reserved three-byte slot is occupied.

Preferred design for evaluation: retain PRES_TIMER as the continuous Attract animation timebase, and store a separate16-bit last-activity timestamp. At navigation, copy PRES_TIMER into that timestamp rather than clearing PRES_TIMER. Before idle comparison, subtract the timestamp from PRES_TIMER using unsigned16-bit arithmetic and retain the existing558-count threshold. Initialize the timestamp at every natural/wake/re-entry Attract boundary. No second per-tick counter or new timing source is needed. The menu handler must preserve its loaded MENU_EVENT in A while recording the timestamp: LDD clobbers A/B, so a naive LDD/STD replacement is incorrect. Luna Extra High independently confirms LDX PRES_TIMER / STX idle_origin is the smaller candidate because it preserves A; prove X is disposable along every subsequent event path before selecting it. Exact local instruction selection remains a fit task.

Code/allocation impact: projected production scope is presentation_runtime.s (initialization and idle comparison) and shared_text_stage.inc (timestamp capture/state alias). Leave the full334-byte actor helper unchanged if this design remains viable. Both modules need one consistent documented state address. Two bytes of persistent state are required; an existing demo/name-only alias is a candidate only after all lifetimes/readers/writers and re-entry initialization are proved. Luna review excludes E8/E9 because the active logo path uses them, excludes the occupied A8FD-A8FF logo slot, and does not qualify B2 gameplay input history as spare. No state address is approved here. Under DP=$00, one extra direct SUBD costs2 code bytes; two direct CLRs for initialization cost4. Those6 bytes are a lower-bound local projection, not total assembled growth: event preservation, repeated entry sites, conditional profiles and branch ranges can add code. Presentation is already1280/1280, so even this lower bound needs local recovery or a revised placement. Resident104 bytes free cannot directly fill a different fixed module without an approved call-boundary change.

Alternatives/edge cases: adding a separately incremented idle counter requires additional update instructions; changing the helper to another animation clock touches the full helper. Removing idle reset violates the requested idle behavior. Using raw global FRAMES changes the timebase and initial phase. Reusing the logo clock slot would corrupt an existing owner. Modulo subtraction can safely measure the short idle interval across a16-bit wrap, but keeping PRES_TIMER alive through repeated navigation also exposes the actor routine's special first6-count startup branch again on wrap; this must be handled explicitly or returned as a scope blocker, not silently accepted. Attract load/wake, Options/Credits return, phase exit and development-profile differences require source review.

Fit/verification consequences: no repair is implementation-ready yet. Finish the read-only ownership/fit report, then approve a bounded repair/card before one build. Preserve helper334, presentation1280 and resident minimum32 in each required profile; no cap/MMU/loader changes. Future verification needs a nonzero-phase Down/release negative control, continued actor progression without restart, the full existing idle interval measured from the last event, natural no-input handoff and clean return from a submenu. Separate short-interval wrap arithmetic from the long-running actor-clock wrap; exact fixture authority and runtime caps must be approved.
